Run ID 20260826T135828Z · generated 2026-08-26T13:58:28Z · auto mode · authoritative card count 90

India raw cotton duty-waiver window — client impact run

Business alert: India's raw-cotton import-duty waiver is active now, not just a future expiry story.

Why it matters: suppliers that directly control cotton-chain procurement can still lower input cost before the 31 Oct cliff, while CMT rows need sourcing-control verification before any financing push.

Immediate focus: separate direct-capture factories from check-only factories, then decide where Air8 should move now versus verify first.

49

4

Facts vs Assumptions

Facts

CBIC-linked project data and runtime cache align on an active HS 5201 window: 11% BCD + 5% AIDC waived to 0% from 2026-06-01 to 2026-10-31.

CP3.5 matched 90 India rows under the strict country ∩ product ∩ COMBO filter: {"COMBO-014": 88, "COMBO-003": 2}.

Live cotton reference from Barchart Cotton #2 (Dec '26): 88.22 USc/lb (+0.27%) on Wed, Aug 26th, 2026.

Assumptions

Imported share, buyer pass-through, capacity headroom, and per-factory procurement ownership remain [UNKNOWN] for most rows.

The user phrased the trigger as an 11% customs duty waiver; project data models the active window as 11% BCD plus 5% AIDC waived to zero, which still needs direct official-text retrieval in a later refresh.

Supplier Readiness Heatmap

24 of 90 clients = direct or likely sourcing-control candidates.

66 of 90 clients = CMT / check-only lane.

15 of 90 clients = [UNKNOWN] revenue band.

49 of 90 clients = US market exposure and therefore an independent UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency gate.

90 of 90 clients = UNKNOWN readiness / no BD call yet.

Strategic Insight

Resilience-Optimized vs Cost-Optimized Sourcing

This run is primarily a cost-optimization window for India factories that buy cotton-chain inputs directly. The resilience overlay matters most on US-facing cotton programs, where shipment eligibility can override the apparent cost edge.

In other words: the event creates a temporary landed-cost opportunity first, but documentation readiness decides whether that opportunity is bankable.

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

FOB row in India suggests some procurement control, but the degree of cotton, yarn, or fabric ownership is still unresolved. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP [MULTI-SIGNAL 🎯]

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: EVT-TARIFF × COMBO-014 → PROD-ARP ✅ VALIDATED. NOT PROD-POOL — revenue bands are mostly too small. NOT detailed pre-buy financing unless sourcing control and ARP track record are confirmed.

Secondary product view: PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C (conditional)

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

CP4b insight notes
What To Ask This Supplier
1. What % of cotton, yarn, or fabric input is imported vs domestic?
2. Who controls procurement for this program — factory, buyer, or nominated mill?
3. Can this supplier absorb a +20% volume increase? Current factory utilisation rate?
4. Are buyer contracts fixed-price or cost-plus; who absorbs the tariff delta?
5. Have any U.S. or EU buyers discussed reallocation, repricing, or accelerated orders during the duty window?

WHAT TO PROVIDE
- Product classification and BOM → confirms HS relevance and cotton share.
- Procurement records / mill invoices → confirms imported share and sourcing ownership.
- Capacity and utilisation evidence → resolves the capacity-risk [UNKNOWN].
- Contract pricing proof → resolves the margin-risk [UNKNOWN].
- UFLPA compliance and origin documents → resolves the shipment-eligibility gate for US-bound cotton orders.

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

SHIVANK UDYOG LTD

EXPAND CANDIDATE (conditional) Verify First COMBO-014 woven EU/UK [UNKNOWN] FOB ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0000743
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

FOB row in India suggests some procurement control, but the degree of cotton, yarn, or fabric ownership is still unresolved. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP [MULTI-SIGNAL 🎯]

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: EVT-TARIFF × COMBO-014 → PROD-ARP ✅ VALIDATED. NOT PROD-POOL — revenue bands are mostly too small. NOT detailed pre-buy financing unless sourcing control and ARP track record are confirmed.

Secondary product view: PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C (conditional)

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

FASCINO EXPORTS PRIVATE LIMITED

EXPAND CANDIDATE (conditional) Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M FOB ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0000865
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

FOB row in India suggests some procurement control, but the degree of cotton, yarn, or fabric ownership is still unresolved. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP [MULTI-SIGNAL 🎯]

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: EVT-TARIFF × COMBO-014 → PROD-ARP ✅ VALIDATED. NOT PROD-POOL — revenue bands are mostly too small. NOT detailed pre-buy financing unless sourcing control and ARP track record are confirmed.

Secondary product view: PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C (conditional)

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

Vertical row in India with woven output and US exposure; this is the cleanest direct-capture profile in the matched set. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Gross opportunity sits at the raw-material layer first, then scales down based on imported share and how much of the saving is given back to buyers.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

If the mill pre-buys cotton inside the window, it may preserve margin and quote more aggressively on later-season orders; if it does not, the post-31 Oct cost reset removes the temporary edge.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win where buyers reward immediate landed-cost savings and where the supplier can document sourcing and scale purchases before the cliff.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Use this as a front-of-queue India opportunity, but keep the recommendation conditional on imported share, contract economics, and US compliance documentation.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP [MULTI-SIGNAL 🎯]

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Now) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.

BOOM CLOTHING PRIVATE LIMITED

MONITOR Monitor COMBO-014 woven multi <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0000822
Action Bucket
Monitor

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

Low-confidence indirect pass-through case with limited evidence that the supplier controls the affected cotton chain economics. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Any captured delta is small unless sourcing ownership is confirmed; the working assumption is delayed or diluted benefit.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

This row may still matter if buyers later reprice or rebalance sourcing, but the event is not yet a direct operating lever for the supplier.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Main risk is missing a future follow-up signal rather than missing an immediate financing opportunity.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Leave in the watchlist and only escalate if procurement control, buyer reallocation, or volume increase becomes visible.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: Monitoring only

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: no immediate financing recommendation. Keep under monitoring until a stronger capture mechanism is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No financing push until event capture is proven

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Monitor) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

VAMANI OVERSEAS PRIVATE LIMITED

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven APAC/EU <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0001161
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

RK IMPEX INC

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0001283
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

What To Ask This Supplier
1. What % of cotton, yarn, or fabric input is imported vs domestic?
2. Who controls procurement for this program — factory, buyer, or nominated mill?
3. Can this supplier absorb a +20% volume increase? Current factory utilisation rate?
4. Are buyer contracts fixed-price or cost-plus; who absorbs the tariff delta?

LYV BRANDS PRIVATE LIMITED

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0001482
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

FOB row in India suggests some procurement control, but the degree of cotton, yarn, or fabric ownership is still unresolved. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

B R OVERSEAS

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0001248
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

FOB row in India suggests some procurement control, but the degree of cotton, yarn, or fabric ownership is still unresolved. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

RAJKAMAL INTERNATIONAL

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0001806
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

SAINI EXPORTS

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0001727
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

- Product classification and BOM → confirms HS relevance and cotton share.
- Procurement records / mill invoices → confirms imported share and sourcing ownership.
- Capacity and utilisation evidence → resolves the capacity-risk [UNKNOWN].
- Contract pricing proof → resolves the margin-risk [UNKNOWN].
- UFLPA compliance and origin documents → resolves the shipment-eligibility gate for US-bound cotton orders.

MACSON PRODUCTS

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0002023
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: direct tariff fit is weakest for CMT within COMBO-014. Use CHECK logic first. NOT PROD-PRESHIP-RT2C — pre-buy capital is inappropriate until procurement ownership is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

This row may still matter if buyers later reprice or rebalance sourcing, but the event is not yet a direct operating lever for the supplier.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Main risk is missing a future follow-up signal rather than missing an immediate financing opportunity.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Leave in the watchlist and only escalate if procurement control, buyer reallocation, or volume increase becomes visible.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: Monitoring only

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

SOLUTIONS.md CHECK

SOLUTIONS.md: no immediate financing recommendation. Keep under monitoring until a stronger capture mechanism is evidenced.

Secondary product view: No financing push until event capture is proven

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Monitor) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

TOOLTECH PACKAGING

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 woven EU/UK <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0002423
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP (only after sourcing-control evidence)

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

Secondary product view: No pre-buy financing until procurement ownership is evidenced

WHAT TO DO

Use the two execution boxes below as the required action plan: first Air8 / BD actions, then client actions.

Air8 / BD actions — Ask, collect, and decide financing
Ask for the last-12-month imported-vs-domestic cotton split because the gross event value is proportional to the imported share. Need to collect: procurement summary or mill purchase records.
Check sourcing ownership and buyer-nominated-material rules because financing should only be pushed where the supplier controls the affected input economics. Need to collect: FOB/CMT order mix and sourcing workflow.
Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.

MARGHOOB & BROTHERS

EXPAND CANDIDATE (conditional) Verify First COMBO-014 woven US [UNKNOWN] FOB ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country
India
Air8 code
C0002618
Action Bucket
Verify First

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

FOB row in India suggests some procurement control, but the degree of cotton, yarn, or fabric ownership is still unresolved. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

Potential benefit exists only to the extent the factory controls imported cotton or cotton-derived fabric and can keep part of the savings rather than handing it back immediately in buyer price negotiations.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

The nearer the factory is to confirmed procurement ownership, the more the window becomes a financing-and-pricing story; the nearer it is to buyer-nominated material, the more the signal weakens toward a monitor case.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can win if the supplier buys its own cotton chain inputs and can move quickly before 31 Oct; loses momentum if buyer pricing captures the full saving or if imported share is low.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Treat as an expand candidate only after BD confirms procurement ownership, imported share, and margin-retention mechanics.

WHAT AIR8 / BD SHOULD DO

AIR8 RISK SIGNAL

Product: PROD-ARP [MULTI-SIGNAL 🎯]

Signal flip condition: imported share, procurement ownership, and buyer pass-through become evidenced; if the evidence is weak, downgrade to CHECK or MONITOR. Independent gate: UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency.

G P HOME TEX EXPORTS

CHECK Verify First COMBO-014 home-textile US <$5M CMT ARP NO Source row 2026-08-04
Country

WHY THIS EVENT HITS THEM

TRIGGER — What Changed

India's raw-cotton import-duty window is active now: 11% BCD and 5% AIDC were waived to 0% for HS 5201 until 2026-10-31. The opportunity is current-state relief first, with a 66-day expiry clock second.

CLIENT EXPOSURE — Why This Row

CMT row means raw-material economics often sit upstream with buyer-nominated mills or fabrics, so the factory may not retain the tariff benefit directly. [VERIFIED ✅ portfolio row] [UNKNOWN] factory-specific sourcing split still needs BD confirmation.

ECONOMICS GAP — Competitive Delta

The relevant variable is supplier-controlled material share, which is currently unresolved; if it is near zero, the duty benefit mostly bypasses the factory.

Gross duty delta = 11% BCD saving + 5% AIDC saving on the imported raw-cotton share [UNKNOWN] Supplier-kept uplift = gross delta × (1 - buyer pass-through [UNKNOWN]) Timing gate = 66 days to cliff; financing tenor should settle before 2026-10-31
Capacity Risk

Can this supplier absorb volume increase? [UNKNOWN]

Margin Risk

Fixed-price vs cost-plus buyer contracts — who absorbs the tariff delta? [UNKNOWN]

COMMERCIAL COMPOUNDING — Second-Order Effect

A delayed yarn/fabric repricing effect could help later, but that is slower and smaller than direct-procurement capture and may never appear if the buyer controls sourcing.

WHERE CLIENT CAN WIN / LOSE

Can avoid losing margin only if material control is broader than the CMT label suggests or if buyers allow some pass-through retention; otherwise this stays a verification lane, not a financing push.

IMPACT CONCLUSION — Decision Language

Keep the row in-scope for commercial screening, but require sourcing-control proof before treating it as a financing-led opportunity.

Decide the financing posture (Verify First) after reviewing OD utilisation, payment terms, and event timing. Need to collect: bank facility letter, utilisation level, and current order calendar.
Client actions — What the supplier should do now
Confirm whether the current season's cotton, yarn, or fabric purchases can still be locked before the duty cliff, because the time window is shrinking. Need to collect: purchase plan and supplier quotations.
Document UFLPA readiness, raw material traceability, and supply chain transparency for any US-facing cotton program, because a cost edge without shipment eligibility is not bankable. Need to collect: origin pack, mill certificates, and supply-chain documents.
Clarify buyer pricing mechanics and volume discussions so the factory can judge whether the event produces retained margin, faster order intake, or only upstream savings. Need to collect: contract pricing clauses, buyer email, or PO amendment trail.
